    1717             : {
    1718           0 :   const float b = angle * (0.5 / M_PI) + 0.5;
    1719           0 :   return ((b - std::floor(b)) - 0.5) * (2.0 * M_PI);

    1721             : 
    1722             : /**
    1723             :  * Clamps an angle between min and max.
    1724             :  *
    1725             :  * All angles are in radians.
    1726             :  *
    1727             :  * This function treats angles as existing in a looping (cyclic) space, and is therefore
    1728             :  * specifically not equivalent to a simple `clamp(angle, min, max)`. `min` and `max` are treated as
    1729             :  * a directed range on the unit circle and `angle` is treated as a point on the unit circle.
    1730             :  * `angle` is then clamped to be within the directed range defined by `min` and `max`.
    1731             :  */
    1732           0 : static float clamp_angle(const float angle, const float min, const float max)
    1733             : {
    1734             :   /* If the allowed range exceeds 360 degrees no clamping can occur. */
    1735           0 :   if ((max - min) >= (2 * M_PI)) {
    1736           0 :     return angle;
    1737             :   }
    1738             : 
    1739             :   /* Invalid case, just return min. */
    1740           0 :   if (max <= min) {
    1741           0 :     return min;
    1742             :   }
    1743             : 
    1744             :   /* Move min and max into a space where `angle == 0.0`, and wrap them to
    1745             :    * [-PI, +PI] in that space.  This simplifies the cases below, as we can
    1746             :    * just use 0.0 in place of `angle` and know that everything is in
    1747             :    * [-PI, +PI]. */
    1748           0 :   const float min_wrapped = wrap_rad_angle(min - angle);
    1749           0 :   const float max_wrapped = wrap_rad_angle(max - angle);
    1750             : 
    1751             :   /* If the range defined by `min`/`max` doesn't contain the boundary at
    1752             :    * PI/-PI.  This is the simple case, because it means we can do a simple
    1753             :    * clamp. */
    1754           0 :   if (min_wrapped < max_wrapped) {
    1755           0 :     return angle + std::clamp(0.0f, min_wrapped, max_wrapped);
    1756             :   }
    1757             : 
    1758             :   /* At this point we know that `min_wrapped` >= `max_wrapped`, meaning the boundary is crossed.
    1759             :    * With that we know that no clamping is needed in the following case. */
    1760           0 :   if (max_wrapped >= 0.0 || min_wrapped <= 0.0) {
    1761           0 :     return angle;
    1762             :   }
    1763             : 
    1764             :   /* If zero is outside of the range, we clamp to the closest of `min_wrapped` or `max_wrapped`. */
    1765           0 :   if (std::fabs(max_wrapped) < std::fabs(min_wrapped)) {
    1766           0 :     return angle + max_wrapped;
    1767             :   }
    1768           0 :   return angle + min_wrapped;
    1769             : }

    4549             : {

    4574             :     /* determine the axis-angle rotation, which represents the smallest possible rotation
    4575             :      * between the two rotation vectors (i.e. the 'damping' referred to in the name)
    4576             :      * - we take this to be the rotation around the normal axis/vector to the plane defined

    4578             :      *   destination vector
